# Follow-up 12 interpretation — AT20

Run `b98b78ee`, harness `65e18e67`: **fail, 80 pass / 0 blocked checks / 2 fail**.
The original assertions are unchanged. The complete case also retains its IDNA,
production distribution and platform resource gaps. [Cache sequence and settings](F12-AT20-cache.json).

[BACKEND §2.1.5](../../../dns/BACKEND.md#215-f10-03--positive-cache-and-ttl-measured-2026-10-31)
provides **F9-HIT / F9-OTHER / F9-ROUND / F9-INFLIGHT**. The measured pin has
independent resolver caches; a fetch is not fallback. Different uniquely matched
responders establish distribution, while fallback requires failure/timeout plus
secondary receipts. Whole-second expiry and older outstanding completions can
change an answer within a stopwatch TTL. No cache minimum-retention promise or
private AdGuard cache flag is asserted.

Both actual resolvers were read before/after: `cache_size=4096` (TEST ONLY 4 KiB,
not the pin's 4 MiB default), `cache_ttl_min/max=0/0`, `cache_optimistic=false`,
`upstream_mode=""`, `upstream_timeout=10`, `fallback_dns=[]`, NXDOMAIN blocking,
blocked-response TTL 5. These settings remain unchanged across this sequence.
The artifact retains actual query bounds, responder, TTL, complete matching
request lifecycle/history, outstanding sets and completion times; separately
sampled authority serial is never a cached positive A record's serial.

The **positive** sequence is primary throughout: `other_ipv4` / TTL 10 with a
fetch, `other_ipv4` / TTL 8 without a new fetch after **1.586580467 s**, then
`private_ipv4` / TTL 10 with a fresh fetch after the 12-second wait. Each positive
probe reports 1 ms. No older outstanding completion exists in this sequence;
the middle sample is an inferred F9-HIT, not client fallback. Its complete
exchange history is retained. This does not repair historical F10-03 or pass
the combined positive/negative assertion.

**F12-03, negative boundary:** initial NXDOMAIN/SOA has **TTL 2** at
`22:26:04.910995661Z`; the next response is positive/TTL 5 at
`22:26:06.139668348Z` (**1.228672687 s** later), from the same primary with a new
fetch. The earlier negative authority completion is `22:26:01.418692885Z`.
This is compatible with whole-second expiry (F9-ROUND); the next query is inside
the sub-second uncertainty window of the remaining TTL. No outstanding request
or responder switch explains it, and metadata alone does not prove internal
expiry versus another invalidation. The combined before-TTL row stays **failed**;
no assertion or cache setting is changed to make it pass. [Exact evidence](F12-03-negative-cache.json).
Future attribution also handles negative SOA TTL and completions from requests
begun between queries; those additive diagnostics have offline tests, not a
replacement live result.

**F12-04, native SERVFAIL:** UDP RR case 01 returns RCODE 2 in **5,001 ms**.
The trace is complete but responder is **unattributed**: primary and secondary
receipts both have RCODE 0 / TTL 5 and complete in less than 2 ms. The client
response matches neither receipt. Both resolvers were contacted; this does not
prove AdGuard generated SERVFAIL, a TTL defect or successful fallback. The
unobserved delivery/client-processing segment and cause remain unproven.
[Original row and whole exchange evidence](F12-04-native-servfail.json).

Run `2be2d32f` and [F10-03](history/follow-up-10/F10-03-cache.json) remain failed
and unattributed. Run 7edb263f's passing fresh cache row remains a separate
[archived measurement](history/follow-up-11/F11-AT20-cache.json).
